❯ dir1: WebStormProjects/ap-executor/src/custom-nodes/ai-agent |  dir2:         
  WebstormProjects/hpoc   doubt: need to design ai-agent-v2 node ( similar to   
  ai-agent ) but it has to use hpoc project. What are lackings? Give top 10     
  points but not too comprehensive                                              

  Searched for 3 patterns, read 3 files (ctrl+o to expand)
  ⎿  Loaded WebStormProjects/ap-executor/CLAUDE.md

⏺ Comparing the two: ai-agent (ap-executor/src/custom-nodes/ai-agent) is a thin,
  stateless Relay node — one synchronous run-agent action that does its own
  provider calls, its own tool loop, and its own tracing, all scoped to a single
  execute() call. hpoc (WebstormProjects/hpoc) is a full standalone agent
  platform (NestJS, Postgres, its own deep-agents adapter, run-engine, memory,
  knowledge, evals, A2A). If ai-agent-v2 is meant to sit on top of hpoc rather
  than reimplement it, here's what's currently missing:

  1. Duplicated agent loop — ai-agent's agent-runner.ts implements its own
     deepagents-style loop and provider dispatch. hpoc already owns this in
     adapters/framework/deep-agents/deep-agents.adapter.ts + host-chat-model.ts.
     v2 needs to decide: proxy into hpoc's run-engine, not re-implement it.
  2. No persistence — every run-agent call is one-shot; there's no run/thread
     object. hpoc has domain/run-engine/{run.service, run-loop, run-recovery, 
     replay.service} + Postgres checkpointing (postgres.checkpoint-saver.ts).
  3. No cross-call memory — context.store is a no-op stub in ap-executor. hpoc
     has a real memory.engine.ts + pgvector-backed memory
     store/embeddings/summarizer.
  4. No knowledge/RAG — nothing in ai-agent chunks or indexes documents. hpoc
     has domain/knowledge/{chunker, knowledge.service} +
     pgvector.knowledge-index.ts.
  5. No governance — the only guardrails today are ap-executor's blunt
     REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS and per-pod concurrency semaphore. hpoc has
     admission.service, budget.service, backpressure.service.
  6. Trace is ephemeral, not durable — agent-tracer.ts just returns a trace blob
     in the action output. hpoc has a real event-log/observability pipeline
     (event-log.service, trace.service, span-projection.service, OTLP export,
     analytics.service).
  7. No agent versioning/catalog — system prompt/tools/model arrive as raw
     per-call JSON props. hpoc models an agent as a first-class, versioned
     entity (domain/registry/{agent-spec, agent-version.service, 
     catalog.service}).
  8. No eval gate — nothing verifies a prompt/tool config before it's "live."
     hpoc has domain/eval/* + deterministic/LLM-judge graders and
     promotion_gates.
  9. Tools aren't sandboxed or catalog-managed — piece-tools just loop back
     through ap-executor's own /api/v1/execute (guarded only by a recursion
     depth check), and MCP servers are raw per-call JSON. hpoc has a managed MCP
     registry with approvals (mcp_server_approvals) and actual tool sandboxing
     (container.sandbox.ts, http-egress.sandbox.ts).
  10. No multi-agent / A2A — run-agent is single-agent, single-turn. hpoc has
      peer-to-peer agent delegation (domain/peer/*, api/a2a/a2a.controller.ts,
      agent_version_sub_agents).

  Also worth flagging separately (not in the top 10 but load-bearing):
  credential-model mismatch — ap-executor's provider auth is a Relay CUSTOM_AUTH
  workaround (single flat secret), while hpoc has a real
  credential-broker.service + capability/credential grants. v2 needs to pick
  which identity model owns provider auth before wiring anything else.

  Searched for 3 patterns, read 10 files (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Good — that maps cleanly onto what hpoc already has. Here's the concrete
  design, grounded in the actual hpoc code (agent-spec.ts,
  mcp-registry.service.ts, tool-runtime.service.ts, catalog.service.ts,
  admission.service.ts).

  1. Provider / Model / MCP dropdowns

  GET /v1/catalog/models already returns [{id, ref, provider, residency, 
  context_window_tokens, status}], org-scoped (catalog.service.ts:218).
  ai-agent-v2's props map directly:

  - Provider dropdown = distinct provider values from that list.
  - Model dropdown (refreshers: ['auth','provider'], same UX as v1) = models
    filtered by chosen provider, submits model.ref.
  - MCP dropdown = GET /v1/mcp/servers (mcp.controller.ts:47) — but note these
    are pre-registered, pre-approved servers, not raw per-call JSON like v1. A
    server must already have been registered + had its tools discovered (POST 
    /:id/refresh) + approved (POST /:id/tools/approve) by an operator before it
    shows up here. That's a real process change from v1, not just a UI change —
    flag it to whoever owns MCP onboarding.

  One thing to flag: test-mcp/{tenant_id}/{mcp_id} doesn't exist today. hpoc has
  POST /v1/mcp/servers/:id/refresh (rediscovers tools, proves reachability as a
  side effect) but no dedicated connectivity-test route, and MCP servers aren't
  tenant-scoped rows — only their approvals are (mcp_server_approvals, keyed by
  tenant_ref nullable). If you want a real "validate connection" button, hpoc
  needs a small new endpoint (e.g. POST /v1/mcp/servers/:id/test → calls
  McpClient.listTools() without persisting, returns ok/error). Worth confirming
  before the UI assumes it.

  2. The run payload ai-agent-v2 sends to hpoc

  POST /v1/runs, Idempotency-Key: <conductorTaskId> (per the ephemeral doc,
  01-ap-executor-ephemeral.md):

  {
    "agent": {
      "framework": "deep-agents",
      "model": { "ref": "claude-sonnet-5" },
      "systemPrompt": "...",
      "tools": [
        "mcp__slack-internal__post_message",
        { "template": "relay.piece", "name": "piece_slack_send_message", "...":
  "see §3" }
      ],
      "responseSchema": { "...": "..." },
      "security": { "dataClass": "internal" },
      "execution": { "limits": { "maxSteps": 100 } }
    },
    "input": { "prompt": "<the workflow's prompt prop>" },
    "threadId": null,
    "mode": "async",
    "delivery": { "webhookUrl": "https://<conductor-callback-target>" }
  }

  mode: "async" + delivery.webhookUrl is the whole point of "ephemeral execution
  + callback to Conductor" — ap-executor gets 202 {runId} back immediately and
  never holds the connection; hpoc's outbox delivers to that URL on completion
  (run-loop.service.ts:2008), replacing conductor-callback.dispatcher.ts
  entirely, exactly as the doc's migration table says.

  Open question worth resolving before you build the callback: hpoc's webhook
  payload is whatever hpoc's outbox emits (run id, status, output — generic).
  Conductor's task-update API expects a specific shape
  (taskId/workflowInstanceId/status/outputData). Either hpoc's webhook sender
  needs a Conductor-specific payload mode, or you need a thin translator in
  front of Conductor. That translator can't live in ap-executor's existing
  dispatcher since the whole point is deleting it — decide where it lives now
  rather than discovering it late.

  3. Piece-tools as hpoc tools — the actual design

  hpoc already has the exact mechanism for this: tool_templates + inline tool
  instantiation (agent-spec.ts inlineToolSchema, materialized by
  admission.service.ts:360). The ephemeral doc even names it relay.piece.
  Concretely:

  One-time setup (ops, not per-run): register a single relay.piece tool_template
  in hpoc:
  POST /v1/mcp/... no — POST /v1/tool-templates (register once)
  {
    "ref": "relay.piece",
    "endpointUrl": "https://ap-executor/api/v1/agent-tools/execute",
    "allowedMethods": ["POST"],
    "pathPrefix": "/api/v1/agent-tools/execute",
    "residency": "internal",
    "defaultEffects": ["non_idempotent"],
    "sandboxProfile": "http-egress",
    "timeoutMs": 30000
  }
  (This table/flow exists in admission.service.ts — I didn't find a
  control-plane route to register a template yet; if there isn't one, that's a
  small gap to add alongside McpController.)

  New ap-executor endpoint (POST /api/v1/agent-tools/execute) — NOT the existing
  /api/v1/execute/:nodeName, because that route's contract ({auth, input})
  doesn't match hpoc's flat args-as-body tool call. This new route:
  - Accepts a flat body: { nodeName, pieceVersion, actionName, credentialId, 
    ...fieldValues }
  - Internally calls the same ExecutionService.execute() in-process (no extra
    HTTP hop) — reuses all existing auth resolution, processors, piece registry.
  - Keeps the existing recursion guard (x-agent-depth header /
    assertToolTargetAllowed) — the doc is explicit this must survive the
    migration.

  Per selected piece-action, ai-agent-v2 emits one inline tool at run-submission
  time, reusing v1's existing field-mode split logic (piece-tool.factory.ts
  already does agent/fixed/leave_empty — don't rewrite it, just repurpose its
  output):

  {
    "template": "relay.piece",
    "name": "piece_slack_send_message",
    "description": "Send a Slack message",
    "inputSchema": {
      "type": "object",
      "properties": { "text": { "type": "string" } }
    },
    "method": "POST",
    "argPlacement": "body",
    "fixedArgs": {
      "nodeName": "slack",
      "actionName": "send_message",
      "pieceVersion": "0.15.0",
      "credentialId": 4821,
      "channel": "#ops-alerts"
    }
  }
  - inputSchema = only fields left in agent mode (what the model fills).
  - fixedArgs = fixed-mode field values +
    nodeName/actionName/pieceVersion/credentialId — merged server-side by hpoc's
    ToolRuntime after the model answers (tool-runtime.service.ts:165), so the
    model never sees or can override the credential or the pinned fields. This
    is exactly ai-agent v1's fixed semantics, just relocated.
  - leave_empty fields: simply omitted from both, as today.

  This means no new tool contract has to be invented per piece — one generic
  template + one instantiation per selected action, and the same field-mode
  UI/logic ai-agent v1 already has just gets repointed at building this JSON
  instead of building the in-process LangChain tool it builds today.

  4. What's actually missing before this is real

  1. A control-plane route to register tool_templates (didn't find one — may
     need adding).
  2. hpoc provider coverage: only echo and a generic langchain.provider.ts
     adapter exist in adapters/providers/. You need confirmed model rows +
     working credentials for Bedrock, LiteLLM, OpenRouter, Sarvam, DeepSeek in
     hpoc's models table — Sarvam in particular has no LangChain integration, so
     it likely needs a dedicated adapter (ai-agent v1's sarvam.provider.ts is a
     ready template to port).
  3. The MCP "test connection" endpoint doesn't exist — needs adding if the UI
     is to offer it.
  4. The webhook→Conductor payload shape isn't decided.
  5. Credential bridging (ap-executor's connection store vs hpoc's
     credential_grants/broker) — you said assume valid, so parked, but it's not
     actually solved anywhere yet.

  Want me to start scaffolding any of this (the new ap-executor
  agent-tools/execute endpoint is the highest-leverage first piece), or keep
  this at design level a bit longer?

✻ Baked for 2m 45s · done Tuesday, 10:20 PM

❯ We can't call conductor directly, better to call a new ap-executor endpoint   
  like /agent-callback/${conductor_id}/${task_id} where task_id and             
  conductor_id} are sent to hpoc as /agent-callback/some_c_id/task_id when the  
  execution payload needs to be send                                            

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 3 files (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Good call — that also avoids reopening the exact SSRF hole ap-executor already
  closed for callbacks. Worth looking at execute-request.dto.spec.ts /
  conductor-callback.dispatcher.ts before finalizing the shape, because they pin
  down something important.

  The callback target must carry 3 fields, not 2

  ConductorCallbackDispatcher completes a Conductor asyncComplete: true task via
  POST {CONDUCTOR_API_URL}/api/tasks with {workflowInstanceId, taskId, status, 
  outputData, reasonForIncompletion?}. And execute-request.dto.spec.ts
  (CBDTO-002) makes taskRefName mandatory too — Conductor reissues a retried
  task with a new taskId, so taskRefName is the only stable key across retries.
  So the endpoint should be:

  POST /api/v1/agent-callback/:workflowInstanceId/:taskId/:taskRefName

  Also worth noting: CBDTO-003 already established the exact precedent you're 
  relying on — "there is deliberately NO caller-supplied URL... a URL in the
  request body would let a caller make this service POST anywhere reachable from
  the pod." Your design keeps that property: hpoc sends IDs in the path,
  ap-executor resolves the real Conductor URL from its own CONDUCTOR_API_URL env
  var, same as today. Good.

  Reuse, don't reinvent, the delivery machinery

  CallbackService.dispatch(target, result) and ConductorCallbackDispatcher are
  already decoupled from how a result was produced — today
  async-execution.service.ts's runInBackground calls them after an in-process
  run finishes. The new endpoint should call the same
  CallbackService.dispatch(), just triggered by hpoc's webhook instead of an
  in-process completion:

  POST /api/v1/agent-callback/:workflowInstanceId/:taskId/:taskRefName
    body: <hpoc's run-completion payload>
        ↓
  1. Validate the caller is hpoc (see below — this is currently unauthenticated 
  as sketched)
  2. target: CallbackTarget = { type: 'conductor', workflowInstanceId, taskId, 
  taskRefName }
  3. result: RunResult = translate(hpoc's body)   // the one new piece of logic
  4. await callbackService.dispatch(target, result)   // existing 
  retry/backoff/metrics, untouched
  5. 200 to hpoc regardless of Conductor delivery outcome —
     CallbackService already logs+swallows permanent failure; hpoc's outbox
     must not keep retrying forever because Conductor is down

  This means you don't build new retry/backoff/metrics logic at all — this is
  genuinely the highest-leverage reuse in the whole design.

  Two things still undecided in translate()

  1. terminal classification. Today, runInBackground marks a ProblemException 
     <500 as terminal:true (caller's fault, FAILED_WITH_TERMINAL_ERROR) and
     everything else terminal:false (retryable FAILED). For v2 you need the
     equivalent mapping from hpoc's error vocabulary —
     admission_rejected/capability_denied → terminal; an upstream 5xx or budget
     hold → not terminal. hpoc's PlatformError codes (seen in
     agent-spec.ts/admission.service.ts: admission_rejected, capability_denied,
     upstream_failure) map fairly cleanly onto this, but the table needs
     writing.
  2. Output envelope shape. outputData today is whatever v1's run-agent action
     returned ({text, toolCalls, stopReason, steps, provider, model, usage, 
     toolErrors, trace?, structured?}). hpoc's run row (GET /v1/runs/:id) has
     different field names (output, error, stepCount, costMicros). If any
     existing Conductor workflow reads specific fields out of a prior ai-agent
     task's output, translate() needs to reshape hpoc's output back into that
     same envelope rather than passing it through raw — otherwise this is a
     breaking change disguised as a callback refactor.

  One consequence worth confirming: v2 doesn't need ap-executor's own async 
  machinery at all

  Since the real work now happens in hpoc's worker, run-agent-v2's handler in
  ap-executor can be a plain synchronous action: one fast POST /v1/runs (mode:
  async) to hpoc, return immediately. No asyncActions entry, no
  RunRegistryService, no per-pod semaphore — those exist to manage a
  long-running in-process execution, which v2 no longer has. What still needs
  Conductor-side config is marking that task asyncComplete: true in the
  Conductor workflow definition itself, so Conductor knows not to consider it
  done until the new endpoint posts a result. That's a smaller, cleaner
  deviation from the ephemeral doc's migration table than what it originally
  proposed (it said delete the dispatchers too — you're keeping them, just
  re-triggering them from a new entry point).

  Security gap to close before this ships

  The new endpoint can force-complete an arbitrary Conductor task with
  attacker-controlled output if left unauthenticated — worse than the SSRF
  CBDTO-003 was written to prevent, because this is the inbound mirror of it.
  Needs a shared bearer token (AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN, same pattern as
  WORKFLOWSVC_CALLBACK_TOKEN) that hpoc sends and ap-executor validates before
  trusting the body.

  Design's solid enough to build now. Want me to start on the ap-executor side
  (the new /agent-callback/:workflowInstanceId/:taskId/:taskRefName endpoint +
  the translate() mapping), or do you want to nail the hpoc-side webhook payload
  shape first so translate() isn't guessing?

       1 import { Body, Controller, HttpCode, HttpStatus, Headers, Param,

✻ Crunched for 1m 30s · done Tuesday, 11:29 PM

› Message from agent-callback-review
  ## Review: `poc/ai-agent-v2` agent-callback endpoint

  I read hpoc (`~/WebstormProjects/hpoc`) to check the "PROVISIONAL" assumptions
  against the real code. They are checkable, and most are wrong.

  ### Blockers

  **1. The DTO rejects every successful hpoc run.**
  `src/agent-callback/dto/agent-callback-request.dto.ts:17` accepts `status:
  'succeeded' | 'failed'`. hpoc's outbox sends `status: 'completed'`
  (`hpoc/src/domain/run-engine/run-loop.service.ts:1940` → `queueDelivery(tx,
  run, 'completed', { output })`, payload built at `:2014`) and `'dead_letter'`
  (`hpoc/src/scheduler/scheduler.service.ts:222`). Only `'failed'` matches.
  Every success → 400 → hpoc's outbox retries 8× then marks the row `abandoned`
  (`hpoc/src/domain/outbox/outbox.service.ts:6,73-95`) → **the Conductor task
  never completes**. Fix: accept hpoc's real vocabulary
  (`completed`/`failed`/`dead_letter`) and map it.

  **2. Auth fails open, on a route that can force-complete arbitrary Conductor
  tasks.** `agent-callback.controller.ts:70` — unset (or empty)
  `AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN` skips the check; `env.schema.ts` makes it `.optional()`
  in all environments; `main.ts` adds no global guard and CORS is `origin:
  true`. Anyone who can reach the pod can complete any `(workflowInstanceId,
  taskId)` with attacker-chosen `outputData` — control-flow injection into
  persisted workflow state, a strictly larger hole than the SSRF CBDTO-003
  removed. The cited precedent is the wrong direction:
  `WORKFLOWSVC_CALLBACK_TOKEN` is an *outbound* credential
  (`callback/dispatchers/workflowsvc-callback.dispatcher.ts:37-45`), not inbound
  auth. Fix: `superRefine` in `env.schema.ts` requiring it when `NODE_ENV !==
  'development'`, so a misconfigured prod pod fails at startup. (The CBDTO-003
  property itself *is* honoured for the destination — path params only, URL from
  `CONDUCTOR_API_URL` — but that was only half the threat.)

  ### Major

  **3. Terminal classification defaults to "retry" for hpoc's most common
  permanent failures.** `translate-hpoc-result.ts:99` requires `error.code`, but
  only one of six `fail()` call sites supplies one (`run-loop.service.ts:240`);
  `:201` ("No framework adapter"), `:243`, `:395` (budget/stop), `:406`, `:660`
  ("Tool is no longer bound") send message-only. All → `terminal: false` →
  Conductor `FAILED` → retries a permanently doomed run, submitting a fresh hpoc
  run and burning LLM tokens per attempt. Fix: classify against hpoc's real
  `ErrorCode` union (`hpoc/src/domain/errors/platform.errors.ts:2-13`) and treat
  message-only/budget failures as terminal.

  **4. Envelope "parity" is mostly synthesised, and the unsafe direction is the
  default.** hpoc's completed output is `{ adapter, text, structured?,
  messageCount }`
  (`hpoc/src/adapters/framework/deep-agents/deep-agents.adapter.ts:303-310`).
  `toOutputEnvelope` (`translate-hpoc-result.ts:51-63`) keeps
  `text`/`structured`, **silently drops** `adapter`/`messageCount`
  (contradicting its own comment at `:38-39`), and fabricates `provider: ''`,
  `model: ''`, `steps: 0`, `usage: {null,null,null}`. Worse: if `output` is ever
  not an object, `src = {}` (`:43-44`) and the task **COMPLETES with `text:
  ''`** — a plausible empty answer Conductor will never retry. Fix: pass hpoc's
  output through under a known key, and fail rather than default when a
  completed run has no `text`.

  **5. The inbound request blocks on a ~31s retry loop with no timeout.**
  `controller.ts:59` awaits `CallbackService.dispatch`, which sleeps across 5
  backoff retries (`callback.service.ts:53-81`), and the Conductor `fetch` has
  no `AbortSignal` (`conductor-callback.dispatcher.ts:54`). hpoc aborts at 10s
  (`outbox.service.ts:71`). So any Conductor slowness *guarantees* hpoc times
  out and redelivers while we are still retrying — concurrent duplicate
  dispatches, unbounded, on a route that deliberately opted out of the only
  backpressure this repo has (`agent-callback.module.ts:7-11`). Fix: detach the
  dispatch (as `async-execution.service.ts` does) and return 200 immediately.

  **6. "Always 200" is false exactly where it matters.** The Zod pipe runs
  before the handler (`controller.ts:54`) → 400; auth → 401; body over Express's
  default 100kb → 413; a hung Conductor → 408 from `TimeoutInterceptor` after
  10 min. hpoc retries on every non-2xx. With finding 1, retry-forever *is* the
  production behaviour.

  ### Minor

  **7.** hpoc sends `idempotency-key: run:<id>:<status>`
  (`outbox.service.ts:68`) precisely so the receiver can dedupe; the controller
  ignores it. The repo's "Conductor's per-taskId TaskResult is the real
  duplicate guard" claim is asserted in prose only — nothing in code or tests
  verifies it. At-least-once is real here.
  **8.** No logging at all on an inbound callback (compare
  `conductor-callback.dispatcher.ts:66`), and its deliveries are
  indistinguishable from async-path ones in `CALLBACK_DELIVERY_TOTAL` (both
  `type='conductor'`). HTTP metrics are covered globally
  (`metrics.module.ts:20`).
  **9.** `controller.ts:58` builds a `CallbackTarget` bypassing
  `CallbackSchema`'s `.strict()` + `min(1)` (`execute-request.dto.ts:25-35`).
  **10.** `output` is `z.unknown()` and lands verbatim in Conductor `outputData`
  (`conductor-callback.dispatcher.ts:48`) — persisted workflow state.
  `structured`/`trace` are unbounded and unredacted; v1 flags this hazard
  explicitly (`custom-nodes/ai-agent/src/lib/common/types.ts:90-91`). No size
  cap; Conductor's output-payload threshold will fail oversized tasks.
  **11.** `durationMs`/`createdAt`/`endedAt` are never in hpoc's payload, so
  `durationFromTimestamps` (`translate-hpoc-result.ts:33-40`) and tests
  THR-030/031/032 cover dead code; `durationMs` is always 0.

  ### Nit
  `controller.ts:72` — non-constant-time compare, case-sensitive `Bearer`, and
  `!expected` treats `''` as "no auth".

  ### Tests
  The controller spec calls `controller.report()` directly, so **the
  ZodValidationPipe never executes** — nothing tests the HTTP boundary, which is
  precisely where blocker 1 lives. `expect.objectContaining` (ACB-001) lets the
  translated body drift freely. No test uses a real hpoc payload, and none
  covers `dispatch` throwing, empty-string token, lowercase `bearer`, duplicate
  delivery, or oversized output. The translate spec is well-structured but 18
  green tests assert a shape hpoc does not send. Also: `CLAUDE.md`'s All Routes
  table and env-var table — both exhaustive by convention — were not updated.

  ### Verdict
  Clean: module wiring, `ProblemException`/`ProblemTypes` usage,
  `ZodValidationPipe` placement, Swagger tags, and reusing `CallbackService`
  instead of new delivery logic. The routing decision (hpoc → ap-executor →
  Conductor) is right, and keeping the destination in env is right.

  But this is **not sound enough to build on yet**, and the reason is not POC
  polish: the contract it translates was inferred rather than read, and the real
  one is in a repo on this machine. As written the happy path cannot work at
  all (1), the endpoint is unauthenticated by default in a real deployment (2),
  and the two directions the design reasons carefully about — "don't tell
  Conductor to stop retrying" and "don't make hpoc retry forever" — both fail
  toward the *worse* side (3, 4, 6). Before the node is built: fix the status
  vocabulary against hpoc's actual payload, make the token mandatory outside
  development, classify terminality from hpoc's `ErrorCode` taxonomy, stop
  awaiting the retry loop in the request, and add one HTTP-level test that posts
  a genuine hpoc body through the pipe.

⏺ Strong report. Before I relay it, let me verify the load-bearing claims myself
  against hpoc's actual code — the whole report hinges on the real payload
  shape.

⏺ Agent "Review agent-callback design + implementation" finished · 4m 30s

⏺ Verified the decisive claims directly against hpoc — the reviewer is right,
  and the headline finding is worse than "provisional mapping needs confirming":

  The happy path cannot work. run-loop.service.ts:1940 sends status: 'completed'
  on success. My Zod enum accepts only 'succeeded' | 'failed', so every
  successful run 400s, hpoc's outbox retries 8× and marks the row abandoned
  (outbox.service.ts:71-95) — the Conductor task never completes. Only the
  failure path's 'failed' happens to match.

  Other claims I confirmed first-hand:
  - Real payload is {runId, threadId, status, output|error}
    (run-loop.service.ts:2014). No durationMs/createdAt/endedAt at all — so my
    duration-fallback logic and its three tests cover code that can never run.
  - Error shape is {message, ...detail} — code isn't reliably there, and hpoc's
    real taxonomy is 11 codes (platform.errors.ts:2-13), not the 2 I guessed. My
    classifier therefore defaults hpoc's most common permanent failures to
    "retryable", so Conductor re-runs doomed agent runs and burns tokens each
    attempt.
  - hpoc aborts at 10s (AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)) while my handler awaits a
    retry loop that can sleep ~31s — so Conductor slowness guarantees duplicate
    concurrent deliveries.
  - hpoc sends an idempotency-key header with a code comment saying "The
    receiver deduplicates on this." My endpoint ignores it.

  The root cause isn't POC polish — I inferred hpoc's contract when the repo was
  sitting on this machine, readable. Labelling it "provisional" documented the
  guess rather than removing the need for one. The reviewer also flags that auth
  fails open by default (unset token skips the check entirely), which on a
  route that can force-complete arbitrary Conductor tasks with attacker-chosen
  output is a bigger hole than the SSRF the design was careful to avoid.

  Two of these are mechanical (status vocabulary, error-code taxonomy, dropping
  dead duration code, honouring the idempotency key); three are design calls
  that are yours: whether AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN becomes mandatory outside
  development, whether the dispatch is detached so the request returns
  immediately, and whether an unmappable output should fail loudly rather than
  completing the task with text: ''.

  Want me to fix all of it on the branch, or just the mechanical contract
  corrections while you decide the three design questions?

        2  import { AgentCallbackRequestDto } from './dto/agent-callback-

        5 - * hpoc `PlatformError` codes that mean "the spec/caller was w
          -rong" — retrying cannot help, so these                        
        6 - * map to `terminal: true` (Conductor's `FAILED_WITH_TERMINAL_
          -ERROR`), mirroring today's                                    
        7 - * "ProblemException <500 = terminal" rule in `async-execution
          -.service.ts`'s `runInBackground`.                             
        5 + * hpoc `ErrorCode`s worth ANOTHER attempt. Everything else — 
          +including a failure carrying no code at                       
        6 + * all, which is most of them — is terminal.                  
        7   *
        9 - * PROVISIONAL: hpoc's actual error-code vocabulary is not yet
          - confirmed. Everything NOT in this set                        
       10 - * (`upstream_failure`, a timeout, an unrecognised or missing 
          -code) is treated as `terminal: false` —                       
       11 - * the safer default, since a transient upstream failure IS wo
          -rth Conductor retrying.                                       
        8 + * The default is deliberately "terminal", which is the opposi
          +te of this repo's rule for its own                            
        9 + * in-process runs (`async-execution.service.ts`: unclassified
          + -> retryable). The difference is who                         
       10 + * has already retried. hpoc owns a durable queue with leases 
          +and its own recovery, so a `failed`                           
       11 + * webhook means hpoc has finished retrying and concluded the 
          +run is over. A Conductor retry does not                       
       12 + * resume that run — it submits a brand-new one and pays the f
          +ull LLM cost again. Defaulting to                             
       13 + * retryable therefore re-runs permanently-broken specs ("No f
          +ramework adapter", "Tool is no longer                         
       14 + * bound", budget exhausted) until Conductor's retry budget is
          + spent, burning tokens each time.

          +he ones NOT listed here are terminal on

          +found` / `invalid_transition` describe a                      
       18 + * spec that will be rejected identically next time, `budget_e
          +xhausted` needs a human to raise a                            
       19 + * limit, and `indeterminate_side_effect` means a non-idempote

      112 +/**

          + scheme is case-insensitive, and a                            
      116 + * client sending `bearer` is not an attacker); the token itse
          +lf is compared byte-for-byte in                               
      117 + * constant time so a wrong token leaks no information through
          + response timing.

      119 +function timingSafeEqualsBearer(authorization: string | undefi
          +ned, expected: string): boolean {                             
      120 +  if (!authorization) return false;                           
      121 +                                                              
      122 +  const [scheme, ...rest] = authorization.split(' ');         
      123 +  if (scheme?.toLowerCase() !== 'bearer') return false;       
      124 +                                                              
      125 +  const presented = rest.join(' ');                           
      126 +  if (presented.length !== expected.length) return false;     
      127 +                                                              
      128 +  let mismatch = 0;                                           
      129 +  for (let i = 0; i < expected.length; i++) {                 
      130 +    mismatch |= presented.charCodeAt(i) ^ expected.charCodeAt(
          +i);                                                           
      131 +  }                                                           
      132 +  return mismatch === 0;                                      
      133 +}

          +KFLOWSVC_CALLBACK_TOKEN (an outbound                          
      100 +  // credential) — the two are not the same kind of secret, an
          +d an optional inbound one fails open.                         
      101 +  // Secret — Vault/ESL only, never a configmap.              
      102 +  AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN: z.string().min(1).optional(),         
      103  
      104    // Wall-clock cap on one async run. Overridable per action v
           ia asyncActions[action].timeoutMs.

           default(600_000),

      251 +}).superRefine((env, ctx) => {                                
      252 +  // Fail closed at boot. The agent-callback endpoint can forc
          +e-complete a Conductor task, so a                             
      253 +  // deployment that forgets its token must not start and serv
          +e that route unauthenticated — a

          + pod than as a silently open endpoint.

          +.NODE_ENV);                                                   
      257 +  if (requiresToken && !env.AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN) {

      263 +        'endpoint completes Conductor tasks; refusing to start
          + it without inbound authentication.',

⏺ Compile error only — AllExceptionsFilter needs injected deps. Since

  less coupled.

⏺ My own test caught a real ordering flaw: Nest runs pipes before the handler
  body, so validation was happening before the auth check — an unauthenticated
  caller got schema feedback. The idiomatic fix is a guard, which runs before
  pipes.

      260  |--------|------|---------|---------|
      261  | POST | `/api/v1/execute/:nodeName` | `ExecutionController` |
            Run a piece action. Returns 200 (sync) or 202 + `{runId}` (as
           ync — see below) |
      262  | GET  | `/api/v1/runs/:runId` | `RunsController` | Debug-only
            lookup of an in-flight/recent async run. Pod-local — do not b
           uild a polling client against it |
      263 +| POST | `/api/v1/agent-callback/:workflowInstanceId/:taskId/:
          +taskRefName` | `AgentCallbackController` | **POC (`poc/ai-agen
          +t-v2`)** — inbound webhook from the hpoc agent platform report
          +ing an ai-agent-v2 run's outcome; relayed to Conductor via the
          + existing `CallbackService`. Bearer auth (`AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKE
          +N`); answers 202 immediately, dispatch is detached |          
      264  | POST | `/api/v1/triggers/:nodeName/enable` | `TriggerExecuti
           onController` | Run a trigger's `onEnable` (webhook registrati
           on / seed polling cursor) |
      265  | POST | `/api/v1/triggers/:nodeName/disable` | `TriggerExecut
           ionController` | Run a trigger's `onDisable` |
      266  | POST | `/api/v1/triggers/:nodeName/run` | `TriggerExecutionC

⏺ Full suite green — 184 suites, 2819 tests. Committing:

  Committed 880a953, pushed to poc/ai-agent-v2, edited PR #150, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ All findings fixed and pushed to PR #150.

  The two blockers:
  - hpoc sends status: 'completed', not 'succeeded' — so every successful run
    was rejected at the validation pipe, hpoc's outbox retried, exhausted its
    attempts, and abandoned the delivery, hanging the Conductor task forever.
    Only the failure path happened to work.
  - Auth failed open — the token was optional everywhere, so a deployment that
    forgot it served an unauthenticated route that completes arbitrary Conductor
    tasks. Now enforced at startup outside development/local; the pod
    crash-loops instead.

  The design calls I made, as flagged: terminality now defaults to terminal
  (hpoc owns its own durable retries, so a failed webhook means it already gave
  up — the old default re-ran doomed specs at full LLM cost); dispatch is
  detached; an unmappable completed run fails loudly rather than completing with
  text: ''.

  Two things worth your eye:
  - I treated dead_letter as retryable — lease exhaustion is hpoc's
    infrastructure giving up, not a verdict on the spec. Defensible either way;
    flagged in the PR.
  - The output mapping no longer fabricates provider/model/usage/steps. That
    weakens the "v1 envelope parity" goal, but hpoc genuinely doesn't send them
    and '' reads downstream as a real answer.

  Two things I'd note beyond the fixes. The tests caught a flaw I'd written
  myself — Nest runs pipes before the handler body, so auth was running after
  validation; that's now a guard. And the root cause wasn't POC sloppiness: I
  inferred hpoc's contract when the repo was sitting on this machine, and
  labelling it "provisional" documented the guess rather than removing the need
  for one. Worth reading hpoc directly when you build the node itself.

  Full suite green (184 suites / 2819 tests), lint clean.

      640 +   * The opaque agent-callback URL handed to a piece that offl
          +oads its work to an external platform                         
      641 +   * (today: `ai-agent-v2` -> hpoc).                          
      642 +   *                                                          
      643 +   * Only built for a `conductor` callback, because the URL's 
          +whole purpose is to carry the three                           
      644 +   * Conductor identifiers the relay endpoint needs. A `workfl
          +owsvc` callback correlates on the                             
      645 +   * `runId` it already received in the 202, so it needs no UR
          +L of its own; and a request with no                           
      646 +   * callback at all (a synchronous test run) gets nothing, wh
          +ich the piece reads as "submit without                        
      647 +   * delivery".                                               
      648 +   *                                                          
      649 +   * Host comes from INTERNAL_API_URL, never from the request 
          +— the same reason the callback

          +chema's own note on SSRF).                                    
      651 +   */                                                         
      652 +  private buildAgentCallbackUrl(request: ExecuteRequestDto): s
          +tring | undefined {                                           
      653 +    const callback = request.callback;                        
      654 +    if (callback?.type !== 'conductor') return undefined;     
      655 +                                                              
      656 +    const base = this.configService.internalApiUrl.replace(/\/
          +$/, '');                                                      
      657 +    const segments = [callback.workflowInstanceId, callback.ta
          +skId, callback.taskRefName].map(                              
      658 +      // Encoded because a taskRefName is author-supplied: an 
          +unencoded `/` or `?` in it would                              
      659 +      // silently change which route the platform's webhook hi
          +ts.                                                           
      660 +      encodeURIComponent,                                     
      661 +    );                                                        
      662 +                                                              
      663 +    return `${base}/api/v1/agent-callback/${segments.join('/')
          +}`;                                                           
      664 +  }

⏺ Full suite green (186 suites / 2848 tests), lint clean. Committing and

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ dist/ is gitignored and untracked for v1 too — consistent. Committing:

  Committed 66990c8, pushed to poc/ai-agent-v2 (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The node now exists. ai-agent-v2 is a real registered custom node, not just
  the callback.

  What a workflow author sees — same shape as v1: Prompt, Provider, Model,
  System Prompt, Structured Output, Tools, MCP Servers, Max Steps. Provider and
  Model dropdowns are served live from hpoc's /v1/catalog/models (model list
  refreshes off the chosen provider); MCP servers from /v1/mcp/servers.

  Three things that genuinely differ from v1, by necessity:
  - No connection/auth on the node. v1's credential existed only to carry a
    provider API key; hpoc's model gateway owns those per tenant now. That also
    kills v1's failure mode where a node could disagree with its own connection.
  - MCP is selection-only. hpoc requires a server to be registered, hash-pinned
    and tenant-approved before an agent can call it, so the v1 "paste a server
    map as JSON" affordance can't carry over. Someone has to own that onboarding
    step.
  - Zero heavy dependencies. The langchain/deepagents/provider-SDK tree is gone
    — the loop runs in hpoc.

  Two executor changes were unavoidable to make the node function, and they're
  worth your attention:
  1. ExecutionService now puts an opaque agentCallbackUrl on the context — a
     piece can't read config or the request's callback block, and I didn't want
     Conductor's vocabulary leaking into pieces.
  2. action-config.json gains externalCallbackActions, a third mode beside sync
     and declared-async. Without it the node can't work at all: it needs the
     callback identifiers, but the async path would fire a Conductor callback
     the instant the piece returns, completing the task with "submitted" before
     the agent had run.

  Full suite green — 186 suites / 2848 tests, lint clean. PR #150.

  Still not done, and I'd rather name it than let you find it: the relay.piece
  tool template has to be registered in hpoc, and ap-executor needs the
  /api/v1/agent-tools/execute endpoint that template points at — without it,
  piece tools resolve to a template that targets nothing. That's the next piece,
  and it's the one I originally mislabelled as "highest-leverage first."

  ┌───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                   │                                                     │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ref               │ relay.piece@1, status active                        │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ endpoint          │ http://127.0.0.1:3100 + prefix                      │
  │                   │ /api/v1/agent-tools/execute                         │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ effects           │ non_idempotent — not cacheable, not auto-retried    │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ residency /       │ internal / http-egress, 30s timeout, 0 retries      │
  │ sandbox           │                                                     │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ methods           │ POST only, max 64 instantiations                    │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ grant             │ tool_template granted to svc:demo-client, not       │
  │                   │ revoked                                             │
  └───────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The grant matters as much as the row: admission checks tool_template
  capability at instantiation, so without it every spec naming relay.piece would
  be rejected.

  Three things worth knowing:

  1. The endpoint it points at still doesn't exist. /api/v1/agent-tools/execute
     on ap-executor is unbuilt, so a piece tool would now pass admission and
     then fail at call time. The port 3100 is my placeholder — ap-executor
     defaults to 3000, which hpoc's API occupies locally. Re-run with
     AP_EXECUTOR_URL=... node --env-file=.env 
     scripts/seed-relay-piece-template.mjs to correct it; the script is
     idempotent.
  2. One template means one contract for every piece action, so it had to be the
     conservative one — non_idempotent covers "send a payment" as well as "list
     rows". If you later want read-only pieces cacheable, that needs a second
     template that only such actions may instantiate; the schema enforces that
     cacheable implies read-only.
  3. I registered via a script, not an API, because hpoc has no control-plane
     route for tool templates — which is deliberate per §18.5 (the contract is
     operator-only). I added scripts/seed-relay-piece-template.mjs as an
     untracked file and committed nothing in hpoc — your main there has a lot of
     uncommitted work in progress.

  Next blocker is the ap-executor /api/v1/agent-tools/execute endpoint that this
  template targets. Want me to build it?

❯ I have a question, the ai-agent node also call http tools why that endpoint

⏺ Fair challenge — let me check what v1 actually does rather than restate my
  earlier claim.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Good challenge — you're substantially right, and my earlier reasoning was too
  broad. Let me verify one more thing before answering.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ You're right, and my earlier justification was too broad. v1 does exactly what
  you say — executor-client.ts POSTs to /api/v1/execute/:nodeName with {action,
  input, auth, merchantId} plus an x-agent-depth header.

  Most of what I cited as blockers dissolves on inspection:

  - "The shapes don't match" — overstated. The model's args can be nested:
    declare the tool's schema as a single input object, and fixedArgs supplies
    action/merchantId/auth at the top level. That composes into exactly
    ExecuteRequestSchema.
  - Recursion guard — solvable without a new route. static_headers on the
    template are emitted by hpoc's sandbox (http-request.ts:122), so
    x-agent-depth: 1 rides along.
  - Auth resolution, actionsAllowed, shared credentials — all already work on
    /api/v1/execute, and reusing them is a plus, exactly as v1's own comment
    argues ("it traverses the entire existing pipeline").

  One thing genuinely breaks, and it's narrow: hpoc merges fixedArgs shallowly 
  at the top level (tool-runtime.service.ts:165):

  toolArgs = { ...toolArgs, ...fixedArgs }

  Piece fields have to live under input. So a pinned field would need fixedArgs:
  { input: {...} }, which replaces the model's entire input rather than merging
  into it. You get agent-filled fields or pinned fields in one action, never
  both. And you can't fix it by putting pinned values in the schema as defaults
  — the whole point of fixed is that the model never sees them.

  So the real choice is smaller than I implied:

  1. Thin re-nesting shim in ap-executor (~30 lines, delegates to the same
     ExecutionService) — no hpoc change.
  2. Change hpoc — deep-merge fixedArgs, or add a nesting arg placement. Cleaner
     conceptually, but it touches every tool hpoc runs.
  3. Reuse /api/v1/execute as-is and drop fixed mode for v2 — a real feature
     loss, since field modes are the thing the design says the node keeps.

  I'd take (1), but framed as a shim, not a parallel execution path. Which way
  do you want it?

⏺ Confirmed — path-consumed args are excluded from the body
  (http-request.ts:98-101). That settles it: you're right, we're not blocked on 
  the endpoint. The existing /api/v1/execute/:nodeName works, and my code is
  just pointing at the wrong place.

  Here's the spec shape that makes it work, with no new endpoint:

  {
    "template": "relay.piece",
    "name": "piece_slack_send_message",
    "description": "Send a Slack message",
    "method": "POST",
    "pathTemplate": "/api/v1/execute/{nodeName}",
    "argPlacement": "body",
    "inputSchema": {
      "type": "object",
      "properties": {
        "input": { "type": "object", "properties": { "text": { "type": "string"
  } } }
      },
      "required": ["input"]
    },
    "fixedArgs": {
      "nodeName": "slack",
      "action": "send_message",
      "merchantId": 1,
      "auth": { "mode": "connection", "credentialId": 4821 }
    }
  }

  nodeName is consumed by the path (so it lands in the URL, not the body), and
  the rest merges into exactly ExecuteRequestSchema. The template changes to
  path_prefix: /api/v1/execute with static_headers: {"x-agent-depth": "1"} to
  keep the recursion guard.

  The two things actually outstanding:

  1. The action schema — this is the real one you're pointing at. My
     piece-tools.ts today expects the workflow author to hand-write each field's
     JSON Schema in the Tools JSON. It should instead resolve the action's true
     props from ap-executor's own metadata (GET 
     /api/pieces/:piece/actions/:action), the way v1's piece-tool.factory.ts
     does — otherwise the model gets whatever the author typed, not the piece's
     real field list and types.
  2. fixed + agent fields in the same action — the one genuine gap. Pinned
     values would need to sit inside the nested input, but hpoc merges fixedArgs
     shallowly at the top level, so fixedArgs.input replaces the model's input
     wholesale. Options: put pinned values in the schema as const (the model
     sees them — weakens the guarantee), or a deep-merge in hpoc. Not a blocker
     for tools with no pinned fields.

  So: no new ap-executor endpoint, and the relay.piece template I registered
  needs its path_prefix/static_headers corrected. Want me to rewire
  piece-tools.ts to this shape and re-run the seed?

⏺ Done — and your instinct saved a whole endpoint.

  Node now targets the existing route. Each piece tool emits pathTemplate: 
  /api/v1/execute/{nodeName}, nests the model's args under input, and puts
  action / merchantId / pieceVersion / auth in fixedArgs. That composes
  byte-for-byte into an ExecuteRequestSchema body, so tool calls go through the
  same pipeline v1's loopback uses — auth resolution, shared credentials, the
  action allow-list. The recursion guard survives the hop via x-agent-depth in
  the template's static_headers.

  Schemas now come from the piece. GET /api/pieces/:piece/actions/:action
  already exposes a per-property jsonSchema (added for v1's CP09), so the model
  sees the action's real fields and types instead of hand-typed JSON. The
  resolved pieceVersion is pinned onto each tool, so a piece upgrade can't
  silently reshape a tool the agent was already configured against.

  Template re-seeded and verified — path_prefix: /api/v1/execute,
  static_headers: {"x-agent-depth":"1"}.

  Two fail-closed refusals you should know about:
  - Declared-async actions can't be tools — they answer 202 {runId}, which the
    model reads as the answer. Same reason v1 refuses them.
  - fixed field mode now errors. This is the one real capability gap: pinned
    values would have to sit inside the nested input, but hpoc merges fixedArgs
    shallowly at the top level, so fixedArgs.input replaces the model's whole
    input. I could have expressed it as a schema const, but that leaks the value
    into context and doesn't actually bind it (nothing validates model args
    before dispatch) — strictly weaker than v1, so it refuses instead.

  Full suite green: 186 suites / 2853 tests, lint clean.

  To get fixed back, the cheapest real fix is a deep merge in hpoc's
  tool-runtime.service.ts:165 — about a two-line change there, versus the shim
  endpoint we just avoided. Want that next, or is agent/leave_empty enough for
  the POC?

  ap-executor is up on :8009 (Node 22, ai-agent-v2: [0.1.0] registered). hpoc
  was already running on :3000.

  Running it end-to-end found four things reading the code hadn't:

  - hpoc needs three identity headers (x-caller-subject, x-namespace,
    x-tenant-ref), not one. Every dropdown and run submission was 403ing.
  - project.externalId has different types on different paths — a string in
    ExecutionService, an async function in the dropdown stub. My merchant-id
    derivation worked when building a tool spec and produced garbage when
    resolving a dropdown. Both now parse project.id.
  - createAction defaults requireAuth to true, so every run was a 400 until the
    action declared otherwise.
  - Tenant refs don't line up: ap-executor says merchant_1, hpoc's tenants are
    merchant-1. The node translates; the real convention still needs agreeing.

  Verified working: provider/model/MCP dropdowns → hpoc; run submission (hpoc
  executed it, returned completed with {adapter, text, messageCount} — exactly
  the shape the translator expects); and the callback relay, which correctly
  fails only at the last hop because Conductor isn't running locally.

  One thing you should decide before this goes further: hpoc cannot send the 
  bearer token the callback endpoint now requires. Its delivery config is
  {webhookUrl} and its outbox sends only content-type and idempotency-key —
  there's no way to attach Authorization. So the manual curl in §7 works, but
  the real hpoc→ap-executor hop 401s. I saw it in the logs:

  POST /api/v1/agent-callback/wf-local-1/task-local-001/agent_step -> 401

  That's the security fix from the review colliding with hpoc's actual
  capability. Cleanest resolution is adding optional headers to hpoc's delivery
  schema — three small edits. The alternatives (token in the URL, or no token)
  are both worse.

  I also added HPOC_API_URL and AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN to your .env (backup saved
  as .env.bak.*), and left hpoc's repo uncommitted — only the untracked seed
  script.

      126 +

          +admin server with 30 tools.

      135 +```                                                           
      136 +                                                              
      137 +Streamable HTTP serves the MCP endpoint at **`/mcp`**.        
      138 +

          +em as a Postman collection variable:                          
      140 +                                                              
      141 +```                                                           
      142 +x-caller-subject: svc:demo-client

      165 +                                                              
      166 +```bash

          +D>/refresh \

          +' -H 'x-tenant-ref: merchant-1'                               
      169 +```                                                           
      170 +                                                              
      171 +Verified: 30 tools, every one `"status":"new","approved":false
          +`. **Discovery is not approval** — a                          
      172 +server that rewrites a tool's description after review is the 
          +attack that rule exists to catch.                             
      173 +                                                              
      174 +### 3. Approve the tools you want (by definition hash)        
      175 +                                                              
      176 +```bash                                                       
      177 +curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/mcp/servers/<SERVER_I
          +D>/tools/approve \

      180 +  -d '{ "toolName": "list_customers", "definitionHash": "<hash
          + from step 2>" }'                                             
      181 +```                                                           
      182 +                                                              
      183 +Each approval materialises a bindable tool `mcp__mock-shopify_
          +_list_customers` **and** its                                  
      184 +capability grant. The hash is part of the approval, so you are
          + approving *that exact definition*,                           
      185 +not "the tool called list_customers".

      194 +```                                                           
      195 +                                                              
      196 +Bound by one team ≠ available to another. `"tenantRef": null` 
          +approves the whole namespace.                                 
      197 +

⏺ The dropdown works — mock-shopify appears alongside probe9jn99.

  Worth knowing: it appeared the moment the server was registered, before any
  tool was approved. The dropdown lists servers, not approved tools, so approval
  state is invisible there — an author can pick a server whose tools will then
  be rejected at admission.

  Getting from "registered" to "usable" surfaced a real hpoc bug. Tool discovery
  failed with:

  Bad Request: Unsupported protocol version: 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18

  connectionFor() set MCP-Protocol-Version by hand while the MCP SDK's transport
  already sets it from the negotiated version — the two arrived comma-joined,
  so every request after initialize failed. No protocolRevision avoids it:
  matching the negotiated value still yields two tokens (2025-11-25, 
  2025-11-25). I proved it by constructing the client both ways — with the
  header, fail; without it, 30 tools. It would have broken discovery against any
  server whose SDK sets that header, which is all current ones. The old comment
  claimed the pin prevented SSE downgrade, but automaticSSEFallback: false does
  that independently.

  After the fix, the full chain verified:

  ┌──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Step   │                            Result                             │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Register │ mock-shopify created                                          │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Discover │ 30 tools, all new, none auto-approved                         │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Approve  │                                                               │
  │ 3 by     │ mcp__mock-shopify__{list_customers,list_orders,list_products} │
  │ hash     │                                                               │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Approve  │ merchant-1                                                    │
  │ tenant   │                                                               │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Catalog  │ all three origin=mcp, status=active                           │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Agent    │                                                               │
  │ run      │ hpoc reached completed                                        │
  │ binding  │                                                               │
  │ them     │                                                               │
  └──────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  That last one is meaningful: admission rejects the whole run if any named tool
  is unapproved, so a successful run proves the approvals landed.

  Two caveats. The echo model never actually invokes a tool — this proves
  binding and admission, not invocation; use google/gemini-2.5-flash for that.
  And the hpoc fix is uncommitted in your working tree, which has unrelated WIP
  — I rebuilt its dist and restarted its API and worker to pick it up. §4b of
  AI-AGENT-V2-TESTING.md has the full worked example.

⏺ Everything's already in place for a Google key — the model row exists with
  credential_ref=test_llm_key, and hpoc supports google, anthropic and
  openai-compatible providers.

  Don't paste the key here. Put it in hpoc's env yourself — it belongs to the
  platform, not to ap-executor, since v2's whole point is that the model gateway
  owns provider credentials:

  echo 'MODEL_CREDENTIAL_TEST_LLM_KEY=<your-google-api-key>' >>
  ~/WebstormProjects/hpoc/.env

  The name isn't arbitrary: the model's credential_ref is test_llm_key, and
  EnvSecretStore maps that to MODEL_CREDENTIAL_TEST_LLM_KEY (uppercased,
  non-alphanumerics → _). It falls back to a bare TEST_LLM_KEY if you already
  have that convention.

  Then restart the worker — it drives the run loop and resolves credentials, and
  --env-file is read once at launch:

  cd ~/WebstormProjects/hpoc
  pkill -f dist/main.worker.js

      177 +                                                              
      178 +  protected override toolParameters(parameters: Record<string,
          + unknown>): Record<string, unknown> {                         
      179 +    return geminiSafeSchema(parameters) as Record<string, unkn
          +own>;                                                         
      180 +  }

      182  
      183  /**
      184 + * JSON Schema keywords Gemini's function-declaration proto ha
          +s no field for. It rejects the                                
      185 + * WHOLE request rather than ignoring them, so they are remove
          +d rather than passed and hoped for.                           
      186 + */                                                           
      187 +const GEMINI_UNSUPPORTED_KEYWORDS = new Set([                 
      188 +  '$schema',                                                  
      189 +  '$id',                                                      
      190 +  'additionalProperties',                                     
      191 +  'const',                                                    
      192 +  'default',                                                  
      193 +  'examples',                                                 
      194 +  'exclusiveMinimum',                                         
      195 +  'exclusiveMaximum',                                         
      196 +  'patternProperties',                                        
      197 +  'definitions',                                              
      198 +  '$defs',                                                    
      199 +]);                                                           
      200 +                                                              
      201 +/**                                                           
      202 + * Narrows ordinary JSON Schema to the subset Gemini accepts. 
      203 + *                                                            
      204 + * Two things bite in practice, both produced by perfectly nor
          +mal Zod:                                                      
      205 + *                                                            
      206 + *  - `z.string().nullable()` becomes `type: ["string", "null"
          +]`. Gemini's proto types `type` as a                          
      207 + *    single enum value, so an array is "Proto field is not re
          +peating, cannot start list" and the                           
      208 + *    entire request 400s — one nullable field on one tool tak
          +es down the whole run.                                        
      209 + *  - Keywords above have no proto field at all, and are likew
          +ise fatal rather than ignored.                                
      210 + *                                                            
      211 + * Nullability is dropped rather than encoded: Gemini expresse
          +s optionality through `required`,                             
      212 + * which survives untouched, so a field that was nullable simp
          +ly becomes optional-with-a-type.                              
      213 + */                                                           
      214 +function geminiSafeSchema(node: unknown): unknown {           
      215 +  if (Array.isArray(node)) return node.map(geminiSafeSchema); 
      216 +  if (!node || typeof node !== 'object') return node;         
      217 +                                                              
      218 +  const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};                    
      219 +  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(node as Record<str
          +ing, unknown>)) {                                             
      220 +    if (GEMINI_UNSUPPORTED_KEYWORDS.has(key)) continue;       
      221 +                                                              
      222 +    if (key === 'type' && Array.isArray(value)) {             
      223 +      const concrete = value.find((t) => t !== 'null');       
      224 +      // A type that was ONLY "null" carries no information Ge
          +mini can act on; string is the                                
      225 +      // least surprising stand-in, and the field stays option
          +al via `required`.                                            
      226 +      out[key] = concrete ?? 'string';                        
      227 +      continue;                                               
      228 +    }                                                         
      229 +    out[key] = geminiSafeSchema(value);                       
      230 +  }                                                           
      231 +  return out;                                                 
      232 +}                                                             
      233 +                                                              
      234 +/**                                                           
      235   * The transcript, preserving tool-call structure when the cal
           ler supplied it.
      236   *
      237   * `request.messages` wins over `request.prompt` because a rea
           soning loop's history is

⏺ It works end to end with the real model:

  The agent called the real MCP tool and grounded its answer in the result.
  That's the whole v2 chain proven: node builds the spec → hpoc admits it →
  Gemini reasons → MCP tool fires → answer comes back.

  Your key was fine immediately. Two more hpoc bugs stood in the way, both of
  which broke every tool-capable run against a real provider — and neither could
  have surfaced earlier, because the echo provider ignores tool schemas
  entirely:

  1. host-chat-model.ts cast a Zod object to JSON Schema without converting it.
     Zod v4 internals went to Google: Unknown name "_def". Now converted with
     toJsonSchema.
  2. Gemini rejects legal JSON Schema. z.string().nullable() → type: 
     ["string","null"], but Gemini's proto types type as a single enum, so one
     nullable field fails the entire request. Added a per-vendor toolParameters
     seam rather than degrading schemas for every provider — the existing
     comment claiming "no per-vendor branch is needed here" was simply wrong.

  One behaviour worth knowing: my first tools run came back completed, billed
  2134 micros, with text: "". Not a failure — gemini-2.5-flash is a thinking
  model, and with a vague prompt plus 10 bound tools (DeepAgents binds 8
  built-ins before yours) it spent the entire output budget reasoning. Naming
  the tool in the prompt fixed it. Related: hpoc never forwards the model row's
  max_output_tokens (8192) to the provider, so thinking models run on the vendor
  default — worth wiring up.

  hpoc now has three uncommitted fixes in your WIP tree
  (langchain.mcp-client.ts, host-chat-model.ts, langchain.provider.ts) plus the
  untracked seed script. I rebuilt its dist and restarted the API and worker.
  All four are real bugs worth keeping, but they're yours to review and commit —
  I didn't touch your branch state.

  in ~400ms. submitted is correct — the answer arrives via callback, not in
  this response. Copy the hpocRunId.

  ▎ Be directive about the tool. gemini-2.5-flash is a thinking model; a vague 
  ▎ prompt with 10 tools bound returns text: "" on a billed run.

  4. Get the answer (paste the run id)

  'x-tenant-ref: merchant-1')

  answer, cost, steps

  proves the MCP tool fired

  All three hpoc headers are mandatory — it 403s without them.

  will fail locally since Conductor isn't running on :8080 — expected.

  One config change was required, and you should know what it cost. The first
  run 401'd — hpoc still can't attach an Authorization header, so the token I'd
  made mandatory blocked its webhook. I used the env schema's own local
  exemption: NODE_ENV=local and AGENT_CALLBACK_TOKEN commented out in
  ap-executor/.env (backup saved). The endpoint is now unauthenticated — fine on
  your laptop, not fine anywhere else. The real fix remains adding headers to
  hpoc's delivery schema.

  Two things worth noticing in that output:

  - task-mock-001 also arrived — the run from before the config change. hpoc's
    outbox had been retrying it against the 401 and delivered it the moment auth
    was relaxed. That's the transactional outbox's at-least-once guarantee
    working exactly as designed.
  - The outputData confirms the translator. text and stopReason mapped, hpoc's
    own fields preserved under hpoc, and nothing fabricated — no invented
    provider/model/usage, which is the behaviour I chose after the review.

⏺ Both answered, but they're in genuinely different states.

  Structured output — works, now verified. It was already wired; I'd just never
  tested it. Confirmed reaching the Conductor task output as its own field:

  { "text": "Returning structured response: {...}",
    "structured": { "customerCount": 9, "summary": "There are 9 customers named 
  A through I." },
    "hpoc": { "runId": "7b08ad41-…", "adapter": "deep-agents", "messageCount": 4
  } }

  One caveat worth knowing: hpoc implements it with ToolStrategy — the model has
  to choose to call a response tool. My first attempt, with 8 DeepAgents
  built-ins plus an MCP tool bound, came back with no structured at all; Gemini
  just answered in prose. It's best-effort, not a guarantee. Same tool-crowding
  effect as the empty-text run.

  Traces — the prop is gone on purpose, but I'd left them unreachable. v1's
  traceLevel inlined a whole trace array into the task output, which v1's own
  types file flags as a PII hazard (at full it carries prompts and tool payloads
  into persisted workflow state). v2's equivalent lives in hpoc and is richer:

  events: run.created → run.started → model.completed → run.completed
  usage : inputTokens 7027, outputTokens 26, costMicros 2173, calls 1
  plus /steps and /tool-invocations with each effect's contract

  But none of it was reachable — the callback dropped the run id, so a completed
  task was an answer with no provenance. Fixed: the output envelope now carries
  hpoc.runId, including on the truncation path, where you most want it. From
  there:

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/events/history "${H[@]}"
  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/tool-invocations "${H[@]}"
  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/usage "${H[@]}"

  The trade v1 didn't make: this survives a pod restart and is queryable, but
  costs a second call instead of arriving inline.

  I did not add a traceLevel prop. hpoc's AgentSpec has nowhere to map it — a
  prop that silently does nothing would be worse than its absence. If you want
  per-run redaction control, that's a real feature request against hpoc, not a
  node prop.

  46 tests in the module, full suite green (186 suites / 2854), pushed as
  80fdf29.

⏺ All verified. Here they are, separately.

  A. Structured output

  A1 — run it. Keep tools off: structured is produced by the model choosing to
  call a response tool, so it competes with every other bound tool.

  curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8009/api/v1/execute/ai-agent-v2 \
    -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
    -d '{
      "action": "run-agent", "merchantId": 1, "executionId": "task-struct-001",
      "input": {
        "prompt": "There are 9 customers named A through I. Report the count and
  a one-line summary.",
        "provider": "google", "model": "google/gemini-2.5-flash", "maxSteps": 6,
        "responseSchema": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "customerCount": { "type": "integer" },
            "summary": { "type": "string" }
          },
          "required": ["customerCount", "summary"]
        }
      },
      "callback": {
        "type": "conductor", "workflowInstanceId": "wf-struct-1",
        "taskId": "task-struct-001", "taskRefName": "agent_step"
      }
    }'

  A2 — read it back from hpoc (paste the hpocRunId):

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/<RUN_ID> \
    -H 'x-caller-subject: svc:demo-client' -H 'x-namespace: demo' -H
  'x-tenant-ref: merchant-1'

  A3 — or see it as Conductor gets it, in the mock:

  Verified outputData:

  { "text": "Returning structured response: {...}",
    "structured": { "customerCount": 9, "summary": "There are 9 customers named 
  A through I." },
    "stopReason": "completed",
    "hpoc": { "runId": "7b08ad41-…", "adapter": "deep-agents", "messageCount": 4
  } }

  ▎ If structured is absent, the model answered in prose instead of calling the 
  ▎ response tool. Bind fewer tools or demand it in the prompt.

  B. Traces

  There's no traceLevel prop — the trace lives in hpoc. Get RUN_ID from
  outputData.hpoc.runId (or the submit response's hpocRunId), then:

  RID=<run id>
  H=(-H 'x-caller-subject: svc:demo-client' -H 'x-namespace: demo' -H
  'x-tenant-ref: merchant-1')

  B1 — event log (closest to v1's trace):

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/events/history "${H[@]}"
  → run.created → run.started → model.completed → run.completed

  B2 — per-step, with latency:

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/steps "${H[@]}"
  {"steps":[{"seq":1,"kind":"model_call","status":"succeeded","latency_ms":2044,
  ...}]}

  B3 — tool invocations (use a run that actually used tools):

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/tool-invocations "${H[@]}"
  {"tool_ref":"mcp__mock-shopify__list_customers","origin":"mcp","effects":["non
  _idempotent"],
   "step_seq":2,"attempt":1,"definition_hash":"ead355e0…","sandbox_profile":"mcp
  "}
  This is the one with no v1 equivalent — every effect with its contract,
  attempt count and the approved definition hash it ran against.

  B4 — token and cost ledger:

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/usage "${H[@]}"
  {"byModel":[{"modelRef":"google/gemini-2.5-flash","inputTokens":16131,"outputT
  okens":23,
               "costMicros":4897,"calls":2}],"ownCostMicros":4897}

  B5 — live stream (SSE, resumable via Last-Event-ID):

  curl -N http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/runs/$RID/events "${H[@]}"

  All hpoc calls need those three identity headers — it 403s without them. Worth
  noting B3 and B4 have no v1 counterpart at all: v1's inline trace had no
  per-effect contract and no cost accounting.

  That latency breakdown is the thing v1 never had — it attributes 4040ms of
  wall time across queue wait, model, tool and human-approval wait, with the
  unaccounted remainder stated rather than hidden.

  The rest of the family, same headers:

  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/traces/<TRACE_ID> "${H[@]}"        # one 
  trace across runs
  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/threads/<THREAD_ID>/trace "${H[@]}" # a 
  thread's turns
  curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/lineage/run/$RID "${H[@]}"          # 
  delegation ancestry
  curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/analytics/latency?hours=24" "${H[@]}"
  curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:3000/v1/analytics/tools?hours=24" "${H[@]}"

  children and lineage matter once sub-agents or A2A delegation are in play — a
  parent run's trace nests its children rather than losing them.

  My earlier answer wasn't wrong, but it was the plumbing (/events/history,
  /steps, /tool-invocations, /usage) rather than the assembled view. /trace is
  what you'd actually put in front of someone debugging a run — it's also the
  closest v2 analogue to v1's output.trace, except it's queryable by id instead
  of copied into workflow state. Worth adding to the testing doc; want me to?

⏺ Good scenarios — they exercise exactly the right surfaces. Both curls are in
  AI-AGENT-V2-SCENARIOS.md, written against the real action schemas. Nothing 
  executed.

  Checking those schemas turned up three things worth knowing before you add
  credentials:

  1. Scenario B is blocked by config, not credentials. google-gemini is
  registered as a piece but absent from action-config.json, so every action
  returns ACTION_NOT_PERMITTED. It needs:

  { "pieceName": "google-gemini", "pieceVersion": "0.1.4", "actionsAllowed":
  ["generate_content"] }

  2. Web search exists, and it's in the piece — not the agent's model.
  generate_content has toolType: "google-search", which switches on Gemini's
  built-in Google Search grounding. hpoc's model gateway has no grounding
  option, so the agent can't search itself — it delegates to the piece. That's
  actually the cleaner design, but worth knowing it's the only real search path
  available.

  3. Scenario A is where the fixed-mode gap actually hurts. insert_row has three
  required fields marked resolvable: true:

  ┌───────────────┬──────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Field     │ Required │                Resolvable                │
  ├───────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ spreadsheetId │ yes      │ yes                                      │
  ├───────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ sheetId       │ yes      │ yes                                      │
  ├───────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ values        │ yes      │ yes (DYNAMIC — one key per sheet column) │
  └───────────────┴──────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘

  v1 handled these two ways: fixed mode pinned the IDs, and companion tools let
  the agent call /api/v1/options/* to discover valid values. v2 has neither — I
  disabled fixed, and companion tools were never ported. So the spreadsheet and
  sheet IDs have to sit in the prompt and be typed back by the model. It'll
  work, but the model can mistype them, and values is a DYNAMIC object whose
  real shape it has to infer from your prompt rather than from the sheet.

  This is the concrete case that justifies fixing fixed — a two-line deep merge
  in hpoc's tool-runtime.service.ts:165. After that, spreadsheetId, sheetId,
  body_type and draft all become pinned and leave the model's schema entirely,
  which also shortens the prompt considerably.

  What you need to fill in: <SHEETS_CRED_ID>, <GMAIL_CRED_ID>, <GEMINI_CRED_ID>,
  <SPREADSHEET_ID>, <SHEET_TAB_ID>, <YOUR_EMAIL> — and a sheet whose header row
  is Name | Email | Created.

  One expectation to set: both prompts name the tools explicitly and in order,
  because with 10+ tools bound, gemini-2.5-flash has already shown it'll answer
  in prose instead of calling anything. If that happens, the trace will show a
  single model_call and no invocations.

  Say the word and I'll run them — or I can do the fixed-mode fix first, which
  would make scenario A meaningfully more robust.
